# SonarPHP 3.60.0.16641 Specification

## 1. Identity, status, and ownership

This specification defines the PHP analyzer bundled with SonarQube Community Build 26.8.0.126808. The released component is `org.sonarsource.php:sonar-php-plugin:3.60.0.16641`, plugin key `php`, language key `php`, display name “PHP Code Quality and Security,” and plugin entry point `org.sonar.plugins.php.PhpPlugin`. Its pinned clean-room source is SonarSource/sonar-php tag `3.60.0.16641`, commit `1edd16ff3f12c7a39d28eb61ee8b1b3ea4c3e875`. The source and packaged analyzer-owned code are licensed under Sonar Source-Available License v1.0 (`LicenseRef-SSAL-1.0`); this is not a GPL/AGPL copy-marker case. [Provenance: `manifest/component-inventory.md`; `manifest/license-map.md`; `sonar-php-plugin/build.gradle.kts`; `LICENSE.txt`; `README.md`]

The analyzer owns PHP file recognition, PHP and php.ini parsing, syntax and semantic models, PHP-specific rules, file measures, executable-line marking, highlighting, symbols, CPD tokenization, PHPUnit report import, PHPStan/Psalm external issue import, and the PHP custom-rule API. The platform specifications own plugin loading, setting precedence, quality-profile activation, scanner report transport, metric persistence, issue lifecycle, duplication matching, and presentation. [Provenance: `manifest/dependency-map.md`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PhpPlugin.java`]

Runtime dependencies are the Sonar plugin API, the PHP frontend and checks modules, SSLR 1.26.0.4194, analyzer-commons and regex parsing 2.29.0.5138, and Woodstox/StaxMate for reports. The plugin manifest declares SonarQube 9.9 as its minimum server line, SonarLint support, and a parent-first classloader. The pinned Community Build runtime is the required integration target. [Provenance: `gradle/libs.versions.toml`; `php-frontend/build.gradle.kts`; `sonar-php-plugin/build.gradle.kts`]

## 2. Purpose, scope, and exclusions

The component statically analyzes indexed PHP source and test files without executing PHP. It accepts syntax through PHP 8.4, constructs project and file symbol information, runs active rules, and emits scanner observations. It also inspects indexed files named `php.ini` and imports configured PHPUnit, PHPStan, and Psalm reports. [Provenance: `README.md`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PHPSensor.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PhpIniSensor.java`]

Out of scope are the PHP runtime, Composer/dependency installation, PHPUnit/PHPStan/Psalm execution, server-side rule/profile and issue state, the platform duplication algorithm after token emission, and commercial/private analyzers. Test fixtures, the example custom-rules plugin, build logic, and integration harnesses are evidence only, not released capabilities. No commercial security engine behavior is inferred. [Provenance: `manifest/component-inventory.md`; `manifest/dependency-map.md`]

## 3. Inputs, recognition, and configuration

### 3.1 PHP files and versions

The language is selected by key `php`. Default suffixes are `php`, `php3`, `php4`, `php5`, `phtml`, and `inc`. Suffix comparison is case-insensitive. `sonar.php.file.suffixes` replaces this list with its trimmed, nonblank values; if every configured value is blank or the setting is absent, the complete default list is restored. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/Php.java`]

The lexer recognizes long, echo, and short PHP opening tags, closing tags, intervening inline HTML, comments, Unicode line terminators and spacing, identifiers, variables, numeric forms with separators, quoted strings, heredoc, nowdoc, and interpolated strings. The parser has one current grammar rather than a user-selectable PHP-version mode. It covers namespaces, imports, classes, anonymous classes, interfaces, traits and adaptations, enums and backed cases, attributes, typed class constants, nullable/union/intersection/DNF types, readonly declarations, constructor promotion, named and unpacked arguments, closures and arrow functions, match and throw expressions, null-safe/member/static access, generators, alternate control-structure syntax, and all ordinary statements and operators represented by its public tree model. PHP 8.4 coverage specifically includes property hooks with get/set bodies, hook attributes/finality/by-reference forms, hooks on promoted parameters, asymmetric write visibility, final promoted or declared properties, and member access immediately following object construction without an extra parenthesized wrapper. No analyzer setting rejects newer accepted syntax on behalf of an older deployment runtime. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/parser/LexicalConstant.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/parser/PHPGrammar.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/tree/`; `php-frontend/src/test/java/org/sonar/php/parser/PHPParserTest.java`; `php-frontend/src/test/java/org/sonar/php/tree/impl/declaration/ClassPropertyDeclarationTreeTest.java`; `php-frontend/src/test/java/org/sonar/php/tree/impl/expression/NewExpressionTreeTest.java`]

`sonar.php.exclusions` is a multivalue project setting whose default is `**/vendor/**`. Matching PHP files are not indexed for this analyzer. Independently, a PHP file is treated as generated and rejected when its integer average line length exceeds 220 after ignoring initial blank lines and a header comment beginning at the file start. An unreadable file at this filter boundary raises an analysis exception. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PhpPlugin.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PhpExclusionsFileFilter.java`]

Main/test classification comes from the scanner filesystem. Main inputs run every active check. Test inputs run only PHPUnit-aware checks; they still receive syntax and symbol highlighting outside SonarLint, but not main-file measures or CPD. S2068 and S6418 are removed from a main-classified file whose path matches `**/test/**`, `**/tests/**`, `**/Tests/**`, or `**/spec/**` only when `sonar.tests` is unset or blank and `sonar.testFileHeuristic.disabled` is not true. A nonblank test-source configuration or disabling the generic heuristic therefore prevents this path-based suppression. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/AnalysisScanner.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/test/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PHPSensorTest.java`; analyzer-commons `TestFileClassifier` contract]

### 3.2 Analyzer settings

| Key | Accepted value and default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `sonar.php.file.suffixes` | Multivalue; default `php,php3,php4,php5,phtml,inc` | Replaces recognized suffixes; blank-only input restores defaults. |
| `sonar.php.exclusions` | Multivalue glob; default `**/vendor/**` | Rejects matching PHP files before analysis. |
| `sonar.testFileHeuristic.disabled` | Optional generic boolean; default false | Disables test-like path classification used to suppress S2068/S6418 on main-classified files; a nonblank `sonar.tests` value also disables that heuristic. |
| `sonar.tests` | Generic scanner test-source paths; unset by default | A nonblank configured value disables the test-like path heuristic used for S2068/S6418; an unset or blank value leaves the heuristic eligible. |
| `sonar.php.frameworkDetection` | Boolean text; default `true` | Enables per-file Drupal, Yii, Laravel, or WordPress adaptation. Only case-insensitive `true` enables it. |
| `sonar.php.tests.reportPath` | Multivalue paths or wildcard patterns; no default | Imports PHPUnit JUnit XML. Despite the singular property name, multiple values are accepted. |
| `sonar.php.coverage.reportPaths` | Multivalue paths or wildcard patterns; no default | Imports PHPUnit coverage XML. |
| `sonar.php.phpstan.reportPaths` | Multivalue paths or wildcard patterns; no default | Imports PHPStan JSON external issues. |
| `sonar.php.psalm.reportPaths` | Multivalue paths or wildcard patterns; no default | Imports Psalm JSON external issues. |
| `sonar.php.skipUnchanged` | Optional boolean; effective default delegated to scanner capability | Forces optimized unchanged-file analysis when true; has no effect in SonarLint. |
| `sonar.internal.analysis.failFast` | Internal optional boolean; default false | Re-throws a per-file analyzer exception instead of logging it and continuing. |
| `sonar.exclusion` | Multivalue glob; empty by default | Suppresses unresolved-file warnings for matching external-report paths; this singular spelling is the one read by the importer. |

Configured project values pass through the platform configuration precedence contract. Active-rule parameters pass through the platform quality-profile/active-rule contract; the defaults in the rule catalog apply when no active-rule override is supplied. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PhpPlugin.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/Scanner.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/AbstractReportImporter.java`; `platform/project-configuration/`]

A report path without `*` or `?` is absolute or relative to the project base directory. A wildcard path is resolved from that base directory. Failure while expanding one pattern is logged at debug level and does not discard files found by other patterns. Report import is disabled with a warning on a plugin API older than 7.2. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/ExternalReportWildcardProvider.java`]

## 4. Parsing, semantics, control flow, and rule execution

### 4.1 Syntax and semantic model

Each parse yields a compilation unit containing alternating script and inline-HTML regions, concrete tokens/trivia, and typed syntax nodes. The grammar is strict at the compilation-unit level: a recognition failure aborts normal analysis for that file. The one localized recovery accepts otherwise unrecognized content inside a semi-complex interpolated-string expression; it is not general error recovery. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/parser/PHPParserBuilder.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/parser/PHPLexicalGrammar.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/parser/PHPGrammar.java`]

The semantic model is built in ordered passes: declarations and scopes; symbol creation; usage association and cross-file lookup; assignment/write-state annotation; then optional framework detection. It represents variables, parameters, functions, classes/types, fields/members, modifiers, declarations, uses, qualified names, owner types, inheritance, method/property information, and unknown symbols where resolution is impossible. A project prepass retains compact class and function data without retaining whole-file ASTs; built-in class data is a fallback for unresolved project classes. Duplicate function qualified names remain a list; the last indexed class for a duplicate qualified name replaces the earlier project entry. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/tree/symbols/SymbolTableImpl.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/symbols/ProjectSymbolData.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/symbols/`]

Framework detection is per file. Imported namespaces beginning with `Drupal`, `Yii`, or `Illuminate` select Drupal, Yii, or Laravel respectively; recognized WordPress bootstrap imports select WordPress. Later recognized evidence in traversal order replaces earlier evidence. Detection changes only rules that explicitly consult it: S100, S1781, S2003, and S4833. When the user overrides S100’s format, its Drupal/WordPress default adaptation no longer applies. S101 does not consume framework detection; it independently exempts a class only when its resolved direct superclass is exactly `yii\db\migration` or `yii\mongodb\migration`. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/tree/visitors/FrameworkDetectionVisitor.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/tree/visitors/WordPressImportDetector.java`; `php-checks/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/checks/ClassNameCheck.java`; corresponding framework-sensitive rule sources listed in the catalog]

Rules are initialized once, invoked once per applicable file with the file AST and symbol table, and terminated once after all files. A rule may emit a precise range, a whole line, or a file-level issue, add secondary locations, and attach a remediation gap. Attribute or comment forms of `SuppressWarnings` accept quoted rule identifiers and suppress an issue when the issue’s starting line lies within the annotated/comment-associated syntax range. `NOSONAR` lines are registered with the platform filter. The recursive visitor stops descending below depth 1500. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/visitors/PHPCheck.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/visitors/PHPVisitorCheck.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/filters/SuppressWarningFilter.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/AnalysisScanner.java`]

PHPUnit-aware rules recognize subclasses of the PHPUnit test-case type, semantically recognized test methods, and a fixed case-insensitive vocabulary of PHPUnit assertions/constraints. Only these checks are scheduled for scanner-classified test inputs. [Provenance: `php-checks/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/checks/utils/PhpUnitCheck.java`]

Regex rules inspect statically recoverable regular string literals passed as the pattern argument to `preg_replace`, `preg_match`, `preg_filter`, `preg_replace_callback`, `preg_split`, and `preg_match_all`; a variable is accepted only when it has one recoverable assigned literal. Delimiters and modifiers are interpreted, pattern parsing is cached per literal, and findings are projected to exact pattern substrings with adjacent secondary spans combined. Dynamic patterns are excluded. [Provenance: `php-checks/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/checks/regex/AbstractRegexCheck.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/regex/RegexCache.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/regex/PhpAnalyzerRegexSource.java`]

### 4.2 Control-flow graph

A CFG can be built for a script, function declaration, closure, or method. It consists of basic blocks, predecessor/successor sets, an end block, and two-way branching blocks for conditions. Return, throw, break, continue, and goto terminate a block and retain a syntactic successor representing fall-through had the jump not occurred. Try, throw, return, jumps, labels, loops, if, and switch create blocks; other statements remain sequential block elements, and ternary internals are not expanded into CFG branches. [Provenance: `doc/CFG.md`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/cfg/ControlFlowGraphBuilder.java`]

The graph is built backward from the end. A for-loop has distinct initialization, condition, body, and update blocks. Switch is modeled as an if/elseif/else chain, and continue inside switch is treated as break. Try/catch/finally conservatively connects the try body to catches and finally, assumes an implicit empty finally when absent, and always retains a possible unhandled-exception route through enclosing finally blocks to end. Cleanup removes only empty blocks with one predecessor and one successor. Consequently, empty multi-successor blocks and a conservative normal successor after finally can preserve infeasible paths and hide some dead code; consumers must not treat CFG reachability as path feasibility proof. [Provenance: `doc/CFG.md`]

## 5. Processing lifecycle, cache, concurrency, and failures

Analysis is sequential within each sensor. First, the symbol indexer visits every PHP input, restoring a file symbol table from cache when an unchanged content hash matches or otherwise parsing and recomputing it. It accumulates project class/function data and writes the content hash and compact symbol data. Second, the rule scanner reparses files that cannot be fully skipped, creates the semantic model against the project index, computes outputs, and runs applicable checks. Cancellation between files stops both phases cleanly and reports cancellation at information level. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PHPSensor.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/SymbolScanner.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/Scanner.java`]

Optimized skipping is enabled only outside SonarLint when the scanner says unchanged files may be skipped or `sonar.php.skipUnchanged=true`. A file must have status SAME or no contrary status and a constant-time content-hash equality match. Full parsing is avoided only if every active check accepts scan-without-parsing and cached CPD tokens can be restored. CPD cache records include the plugin version and separate token-data/string-table entries. Cache absence, a hash read failure, or any check refusing the shortcut falls back to parsing. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/Scanner.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/AnalysisScanner.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/metrics/CpdVisitor.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/cache/`]

A symbol-prepass parse failure is debug-logged and omits that file’s declarations from the project index, but later files and the rule pass continue. A rule-pass parse failure logs warnings, emits S2260 at the recognition line when that rule is active, records a scanner analysis error with the parser message, and emits no normal rules, measures, highlighting, symbols, or CPD for that file. An interrupted parse becomes cancellation. Other per-file exceptions are warned and skipped unless fail-fast is enabled. Rule termination failures are warned without aborting termination of remaining rules. A rule-caused `StackOverflowError` is logged with the check and file and then rethrown; because the file loop catches exceptions rather than errors, it escapes the loop and aborts the PHP sensor regardless of `sonar.internal.analysis.failFast`. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/SymbolScanner.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/AnalysisScanner.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/Scanner.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/PHPAnalyzer.java`]

`php.ini` analysis separately selects every indexed path matching `**/php.ini` when a PHP language is present in the project. A php.ini parse failure warns and skips only that file. Findings use the directive line when one exists and file scope when a required directive is absent. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PhpIniSensor.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/ini/PhpIniParser.java`]

## 6. Outputs: metrics, lines, highlighting, symbols, and CPD

For every successfully parsed main file outside SonarLint, the analyzer emits these core measures: `ncloc`, `comment_lines`, `classes`, `functions`, `statements`, `cognitive_complexity`, and `complexity`. It also writes `ncloc_data` and `executable_lines_data`. Class count includes classes, interfaces, and traits but not enums; function count includes functions, methods, closures, and arrow functions. Statement count includes declarations and the enumerated control/jump/expression/global/static/trait-use statement nodes. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/AnalysisScanner.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/metrics/MetricsVisitor.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/metrics/CounterVisitor.java`]

NCLOC is the set of source lines occupied by non-EOF syntax tokens after omitting PHP opening tags. Comment lines are nonblank comment-content lines; a line containing `NOSONAR` is registered for suppression and is not counted as a comment line by this visitor. Executable lines are the starting lines of return, continue, break, goto, throw, empty, global, static, try, switch, if, while, do-while, foreach, for, expression, and unset statements. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/metrics/LineVisitor.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/metrics/CommentLineVisitor.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/metrics/ExecutableLineVisitor.java`]

Cyclomatic complexity increments for each function-like declaration, case, loop, if/elseif, ternary, and conditional boolean operator. File cognitive complexity sums script complexity and separately computed function-like complexity; it adds nesting-sensitive increments for structured flow, catches, ternaries, and nested functions, non-nesting increments for else/elseif and labeled jumps, and sequence-sensitive increments for logical operators. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/metrics/ComplexityVisitor.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/metrics/CognitiveComplexityVisitor.java`]

Syntax highlighting marks PHP keywords and reserved magic variables as keywords, documentation comments as structured comments, other comments as comments, string portions as strings, and numeric literals as constants. Symbol output creates a declaration and references for every resolved file symbol. Both outputs are saved for parsed main and test inputs outside SonarLint. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/highlighter/SyntaxHighlighterVisitor.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/highlighter/SymbolHighlighter.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/AnalysisScanner.java`]

CPD output is main-file only and excludes opening tags, inline HTML, namespace/use statements, attributes, and EOF. Numeric literals normalize to `$NUMBER`; regular strings, nowdocs, and expandable-string text normalize to `$CHARS`; all other tokens retain their text and exact range. The analyzer emits tokens only; the platform compute engine owns clone matching and duplication measures. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/metrics/CpdVisitor.java`; `platform/compute-engine/`]

In SonarLint, the analyzer runs rules but omits measures, CPD, syntax highlighting, and symbol output, excludes S5911, disables optimized skipping, and does not register PHPUnit or external-report sensors. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PhpPlugin.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/AnalysisScanner.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/Scanner.java`]

## 7. PHPUnit coverage and test execution reports

`sonar.php.coverage.reportPaths` accepts PHPUnit coverage XML rooted at `coverage`. Only the first `project` is consumed. File entries may occur directly under that project or under packages. Each line requires integer `num` and `count` attributes; the optional type is read but does not create branch coverage. Paths are mapped to indexed files, and valid lines from 1 through the source file’s line count become line-hit observations. Out-of-range lines warn and are skipped. An empty report warns; an unresolved source path is skipped and included in one bounded warning. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpunit/CoverageFileParserForPhpUnit.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpunit/CoverageResultImporter.java`]

`sonar.php.tests.reportPath` accepts PHPUnit’s JUnit-style XML rooted at `testsuites`, including nested suites. Suites with a file attribute associate descendant cases with that file; container suites retain nested structure. Test-case error, failure, and skipped children determine status. Per indexed PHP test file the importer writes `skipped_tests`, `test_execution_time` in truncated milliseconds, `tests` as total minus skipped, `test_errors`, and `test_failures`. A target must be indexed as language PHP and type TEST. Missing or invalid time becomes zero. An empty arranged result warns. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpunit/JUnitLogParserForPhpUnit.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpunit/TestFileReport.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpunit/TestResultImporter.java`]

If `sonar.tests` is absent, the sensor checks the first 1024 bytes of PHP inputs for the fully qualified PHPUnit test-case name and warns when undeclared test cases are found. Read failures during this heuristic are debug-only. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpunit/PhpUnitSensor.java`]

The XML factory disables DTDs, validation, entity replacement, and external entities. A malformed or unreadable XML report produces a warning and contributes nothing, while other configured reports continue. Report path mapping first accepts an already-known path; otherwise it normalizes separators and removes leading path components until an indexed path is found, then reuses that offset for later paths when possible. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpunit/JUnitLogParserForPhpUnit.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/ExternalReportFileHandler.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/AbstractReportImporter.java`]

## 8. PHPStan and Psalm external reports

The external repositories are `external_phpstan` and `external_psalm`, both language `php`. Sensors run only when their respective path property is present. A report issue must have a nonblank file path and message and must map to an indexed input; otherwise it is skipped. Missing mappings are summarized in a warning. Each external issue has a fixed five-minute remediation effort. Supplied legacy types `BUG`, `VULNERABILITY`, `SECURITY_HOTSPOT`, and `CODE_SMELL` are accepted; anything else defaults to code smell. Supplied severities `INFO`, `MINOR`, `MAJOR`, `CRITICAL`, and `BLOCKER` are accepted; anything else defaults to major. These map respectively to reliability/security/maintainability and low/medium/high impacts on supported Community Build runtimes. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/ExternalIssuesSensor.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/AbstractExternalRulesDefinition.java`]

PHPStan JSON is an object whose `files` member, when it is an object, maps file paths to objects containing `messages` arrays. Each message supplies `line` and `message`; a suffix beginning with “(in context of …)” is removed from a trait-context path. PHPStan has no per-message imported rule identifier, type, severity, or column; every imported finding uses `external_phpstan:phpstan.finding`, defaults to major code smell at the supplied line, and has reliability-medium rule metadata on the pinned runtime. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpstan/PhpStanJsonReportReader.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/resources/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpstan/rules.json`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/test/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpstan/PhpStanRulesDefinitionTest.java`]

Psalm JSON is an object with an `issues` array. Each accepted entry has `primaryLocation.filePath`, `primaryLocation.message`, and optionally a `textRange` with start/end lines and columns, plus optional top-level `ruleId`, type, and severity. A known `ruleId` selects the exact external rule; missing or unknown identifiers use `external_psalm:psalm.finding`. A valid start column creates a character range, with a valid end pair expanding it; an invalid or absent column falls back to the whole start line, and an absent start line leaves file scope. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/psalm/PsalmJsonReportReader.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/ExternalIssuesSensor.java`]

A malformed JSON root/type, parse error, or unreadable report warns that no issue is imported from that report. Unknown individual Psalm rule IDs do not fail the report; they use the fallback key. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/ExternalIssuesSensor.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/AbstractReportImporter.java`]

## 9. Custom-rule API

A scanner-side extension implements `PHPCustomRuleRepository`, returns a unique repository key and a list of annotated check classes, and is injected into the PHP sensor. Custom checks and built-in checks share parsing, semantic context, scheduling, suppression, issue conversion, and lifecycle. The public check contract supplies one-time initialization/termination, per-file analysis, an opt-in unchanged-file shortcut, current AST, source-file identity, working directory, cache context, symbol table, and detected framework. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/visitors/PHPCustomRuleRepository.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/visitors/PHPCheck.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PHPChecks.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/PHPSensor.java`]

A check may use the full recursive visitor or subscribe to selected tree kinds. It may report a tree range, a start-tree/end-tree range, an explicit line/column location, a whole line, or a whole file, with optional secondary locations and cost. The public AST covers lexical, declaration, expression, statement, CFG, and symbol contracts; compatibility-sensitive consumers must compile against this released API. Deprecated PHP 8 transition accessors remain documented as deprecated, while the former `PHPCustomRulesDefinition` contract has been removed since 3.15. The explicit-location issue endpoint is available since 3.32. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/visitors/CheckContext.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/visitors/PHPVisitorCheck.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/visitors/PHPSubscriptionCheck.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/tree/`; `README.md`]

A custom check that returns true from scan-without-parsing asserts that it needs no AST work for an unchanged file; returning false forces normal parsing. Failures follow ordinary per-file and fail-fast behavior. Custom repositories do not define server persistence or quality-profile precedence. [Provenance: `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/api/visitors/PHPCheck.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/AnalysisScanner.java`; `platform/plugin-extension-runtime/`; `platform/project-configuration/`]

## 10. Native rule catalog

The `php` repository contains exactly 245 registered rules at this release. Every row below states the rule’s trigger, all source-observed applicability restrictions and exceptions, exact primary and secondary issue locations, custom remediation-gap behavior, parameters, and semantic provenance. Global prerequisites still apply: a rule must be active; the file must survive language recognition, scanner indexing, and PHP exclusions; parsing must succeed; and any semantic fact required by a rule must resolve. “Default” means active in the built-in `Sonar way` profile. Legacy type/severity, clean-code attribute, and impact metadata are preserved per key. [Provenance: `php-checks/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/checks/CheckList.java`; `php-checks/src/main/resources/org/sonar/l10n/php/rules/php/Sonar_way_profile.json`; per-row sources and matching `<key>.json` metadata]

| Rule key | Default | Classification | Detection semantics | Rule-specific exclusions and limits | Issue contract | Parameters and defaults | Semantic source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `php:NoSonar` | no | CODE_SMELL / Major; CLEAR; MAINTAINABILITY=MEDIUM | Inspect every physical line within each syntax comment. Create one issue for each such line containing the case-sensitive contiguous substring NOSONAR, regardless of where it appears or how many times it occurs on that line. Line numbering begins at the comment’s starting line and advances across CR, LF, or CRLF separators. | Text outside comment trivia, including string contents and ordinary source tokens, is not inspected. Different casing, interrupted text, and comment lines without the exact contiguous substring are excluded. Multiple occurrences on one comment line still produce only one issue for that line. | Primary: The physical source line within the comment that contains NOSONAR. Secondary: None. Remediation gap: Default rule remediation. | none | `php-checks/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/checks/NoSonarCheck.java` |
| `php:S100` | yes | CODE_SMELL / Minor; IDENTIFIABLE; MAINTAINABILITY=LOW | Inspect every named function and every method whose override status is definitively false. Report a declaration when its complete name does not match the active format regex and is not a recognized PHP magic method. The default format is ^[a-z][a-zA-Z0-9]*$; with an unchanged default under Drupal or WordPress, ^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$ is used instead. | Names matching the active regex and recognized magic methods are excluded. Methods confirmed or potentially unresolved as overrides are skipped; standalone functions are always eligible. Anonymous callables without a declared name are outside scope. Drupal and WordPress alter the regex only when format remains at its default; an explicitly configured format always takes precedence and must be valid. | Primary: The declared function or method name identifier. Secondary: None. Remediation gap: Default rule remediation. | format=`^[a-z][a-zA-Z0-9]*$`; exact registry §10.A.1 | `php-checks/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/checks/FunctionNameCheck.java` |

Matching is against the entire identifier rather than a substring. | Interfaces, traits, enums, and anonymous classes are outside the visited declaration kind. A class is exempt when its resolved direct superclass has the qualified name yii\db\migration or yii\mongodb\migration. If no superclass is present or its identity cannot resolve to either exact name, the class is checked normally. The configured regex must be valid. | Primary: The declared class name identifier. It does not apply separate limits to classes, functions, or other regions. | Primary: The entire PHP file. Secondary: None. ### 10.A Exact fixed registries used by native rules

These lists are normative behavior data, not implementation examples. Names are compared with the case and resolution rules stated in the corresponding catalog row. Each row that depends on a registry references the subsection below. [Provenance: pinned rule sources; the two S6437 JSON resources; exact analyzer-commons 2.29.0.5138 source artifact, SHA-256 `33165e7f32e3cbce84111d100e97bc821909582373e372640dec8022ffbd2a22`, <https://repo1.maven.org/maven2/org/sonarsource/analyzer-commons/sonar-analyzer-commons/2.29.0.5138/sonar-analyzer-commons-2.29.0.5138-sources.jar>]


#### 10.A.1 Shared names for S100, S117, S1600, and S2043

S100 magic-method exemptions (17):
- `__construct`, `__destruct`, `__call`, `__callStatic`, `__get`, `__set`, `__isset`, `__unset`, `__sleep`, `__wakeup`
- `__toString`, `__invoke`, `__set_state`, `__clone`, `__debugInfo`, `__serialize`, `__unserialize`

S117 recognized superglobals (9):
- `$GLOBALS`, `$_SERVER`, `$_GET`, `$_POST`, `$_FILES`, `$_COOKIE`, `$_SESSION`, `$_REQUEST`, `$_ENV`

S2043 directly reported superglobals (7):
- `$_COOKIE`, `$_ENV`, `$_FILES`, `$_GET`, `$_POST`, `$_REQUEST`, `$_SERVER`

S1600 deprecated-to-replacement pairs (8):
- `$HTTP_SERVER_VARS → $_SERVER`, `$HTTP_GET_VARS → $_GET`, `$HTTP_POST_VARS → $_POST`, `$HTTP_POST_FILES → $_FILES`, `$HTTP_SESSION_VARS → $_SESSION`, `$HTTP_ENV_VARS → $_ENV`, `$HTTP_COOKIE_VARS → $_COOKIE`, `$php_errormsg → error_get_last()`

#### 10.A.2 S836 scope and predefined-variable registry

Calls that make the current scope indeterminate (8):
- `eval`, `extract`, `parse_str`, `preg_replace`, `include`, `include_once`, `require`, `require_once`

Predefined variables (13):
- `$_COOKIE`, `$_ENV`, `$_FILES`, `$_GET`, `$_POST`, `$_REQUEST`, `$_SERVER`, `$_SESSION`, `$GLOBALS`, `$HTTP_RAW_POST_DATA`
- `$HTTP_RESPONSE_HEADER`, `$PHP_ERRORMSG`, `$THIS`

For argument-read analysis, S836 uses every S2201 name in §10.A.7, adds `echo`, and removes `isset`.

#### 10.A.3 S1192 duplication allowances

Translation-call exclusions (11):
- `__`, `_e`, `t`, `esc_html__`, `esc_html_e`, `esc_attr__`, `esc_attr_e`, `_n`, `_x`, `_nx`
- `_ex`

A normalized duplicate is allowed when the whole value is identifier-like text beginning with an ASCII letter or underscore and continuing only with ASCII letters, digits, underscore, period, colon, comma, vertical bar, or hyphen. It is also allowed when the whole value is HTML-like content composed of text outside angle brackets and one or more complete opening, closing, or self-closing tags; tag and attribute names begin with an ASCII letter and continue with ASCII letters, digits, hyphen, underscore, colon, or period; attributes may be bare or assigned quoted or unquoted values; and a syntactically partial tag/attribute may occur only at a literal boundary. [Provenance: `StringLiteralDuplicatedCheck.java`]

#### 10.A.4 S1200 coupling exclusions

Recognized documentation tags (6):
- `@var`, `@global`, `@staticvar`, `@throws`, `@param`, `@return`

Excluded scalar/pseudo-types (12):
- `integer`, `int`, `double`, `float`, `string`, `array`, `object`, `boolean`, `bool`, `binary`
- `null`, `mixed`

#### 10.A.5 S1607 abort calls
- `marktestskipped`, `marktestincomplete`

#### 10.A.6 S2077 SQL call and argument selectors

Procedural calls (10):
- `mssql_query: query@0`, `mysql_query: query@0`, `mysql_db_query: query@1`, `mysql_unbuffered_query: query@0`
- `pg_send_query: query@1`, `mysqli_query: query@1`, `mysqli_real_query: query@1`, `mysqli_multi_query: query@1`
- `mysqli_send_query: query@1`, `pg_query: query@0 when one argument, query@1 when two arguments`

Resolved member calls (7):
- `PDO::exec: statement@0`, `PDO::query: statement@0`, `PDO::prepare: statement@0`, `mysqli::query: query@0`
- `mysqli::real_query: query@0`, `mysqli::multi_query: query@0`, `mysqli::send_query: query@0`

#### 10.A.8 PHPUnit assertion and constraint vocabulary

- `assertCount`, `assertNotCount`, `assertEquals`, `assertEqualsCanonicalizing`, `assertEqualsIgnoringCase`, `assertEqualsWithDelta`, `assertNotEquals`, `assertNotEqualsCanonicalizing`, `assertNotEqualsIgnoringCase`, `assertNotEqualsWithDelta`

#### 10.A.14 S5332 clear-text protocols and safe literal hosts

Clear-text-to-secure alternatives (17):
- `HTTP→HTTPS`, `FTP→SFTP, SCP or FTPS`, `WS→WSS`, `Telnet→SSH`, `Gopher→HTTPS`, `TFTP→SFTP`
- `SMTP→SMTPS`, `LDAP→LDAPS`, `IMAP→IMAPS`, `POP3→POP3S`, `AMQP→AMQPS`, `MQTT→MQTTS`
- `SIP→SIPS`, `RTMP→RTMPS`, `IRC→IRCS`, `NNTP→NNTPS`, `STOMP→STOMPS`

Internal/nonpublic hosts accepted without TLS:
- `localhost`, `IPv4 loopback 127 with one to three following numeric octets`, `bracketed IPv6 loopback ::1 including zero-expanded forms`, `IPv4 link-local 169.254.x.x`
- `[fd00:ec2::254]`, `168.63.129.16`, `100.100.100.200`, `metadata.google.internal`
- `metadata.internal`, `host.docker.internal`, `gateway.docker.internal`, `hosts ending .svc.cluster.local`

Namespace URI authorities accepted without TLS:
- `www.w3.org`, `schemas.android.com`, `schemas.microsoft.com`, `schemas.xmlsoap.org`, `www.sap.com`, `www.opengis.net`, `hl7.org`
- `unitsofmeasure.org`, `purl.org`, `docs.oasis-open.org`, `xmlns.com`, `json-ld.org`, `schema.org`, `www.springframework.org`
- `maven.apache.org`, `dublincore.org`, `ogp.me`, `xml.apache.org`, `schemas.openxmlformats.org`, `rdfs.org`, `schemas.google.com`
- `a9.com`, `ns.adobe.com`, `ltsc.ieee.org`, `docbook.org`, `graphml.graphdrawing.org`, `json-schema.org`

Documentation hosts accepted without TLS are `example.com`, `example.net`, and `example.org` including subdomains, plus any host ending in `.example`, `.test`, or `.localhost`. [Provenance: analyzer-commons `CleartextProtocolFilter.java` from the exact artifact identified above]

Before reporting a selected literal, S6437 excludes case-insensitive exact values `hunter2`, `letmein`, `abc123`, `changeme`, `changeit`, `unknown`, `optional`, `enabled`, `disabled`, `string`, `random`, `token`. It also excludes values recognized as short/fake, password-like or repeated/masked values; template, interpolation, environment/configuration, command-substitution, or bracket placeholders; encrypted markers; external secret-store references; filesystem paths with at least three segments; semantic versions; and peer-annotated lockfile versions. [Provenance: analyzer-commons `SecretClassifier.java` from the exact artifact identified above]

## 11. External rule-key catalog

The following external keys are shipped definitions, not detections performed by SonarPHP. Their detection contract is report-driven: an issue is assigned to the key only when a configured report supplies the corresponding identifier and accepted location/message data. They have no analyzer parameters and use report-derived locations. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/resources/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpstan/rules.json`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/resources/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/psalm/rules.json`]

- `external_phpstan:phpstan.finding` — the sole PHPStan rule; all accepted PHPStan messages use it. Its clean-code attribute is `LOGICAL` and its impact is `RELIABILITY=MEDIUM`.

The Psalm repository contains exactly 266 keys. The suffix is Psalm’s public issue identifier; each full key is `external_psalm:<suffix>`. `external_psalm:psalm.finding` is the fallback for missing, custom, or unknown identifiers. The groups below preserve every key’s complete shipped legacy type, clean-code attribute, and impact quality/severity tuple; keys within one group have identical metadata. [Provenance: packaged Psalm `rules.json`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/test/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/psalm/PsalmRulesDefinitionTest.java`]

## 12. Security and trust boundaries

PHP source, php.ini content, and configured reports are untrusted inputs. The analyzer parses but does not execute PHP, invoke frameworks, follow include/require paths, load Composer code, run regexes through PHP, or connect to services named in source. Semantic resolution is limited to indexed declarations and bundled built-in symbol data. Security rules report statically recognizable constructs; inability to resolve a dynamic call or value is not a license to execute it. [Provenance: parser, symbol, regex, and rule sources indexed above]

Report files are read only from operator-configured paths. Paths inside reports are used to locate already-indexed scanner files; they do not authorize arbitrary source-file reads. XML DTDs and external entities are disabled. Malformed reports are isolated per report. [INFERENCE] The JSON readers expose no analyzer-specific file-size limit, so deployment resource controls remain a platform responsibility. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/ExternalReportFileHandler.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/reports/phpunit/JUnitLogParserForPhpUnit.java`; JSON readers listed above]

The cache is scanner-provided and version-coupled. Content hashes guard reuse, and cache deserializers use the current plugin version. Cache data is an optimization, not authoritative source; invalid/missing data must fall back to parsing or omit only the affected shortcut. [Provenance: `sonar-php-plugin/src/main/java/org/sonar/plugins/php/Scanner.java`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/cache/`; `php-frontend/src/main/java/org/sonar/php/metrics/CpdVisitor.java`]

## 13. Compatibility and limits

- PHP syntax is accepted through 8.4 with the modern constructs described above; there is no PHP-runtime-version setting or compatibility diagnostic mode. [Provenance: `README.md`; plugin property definitions; grammar sources]
- Main recursive visitors do not descend beyond 1500 nested tree levels. CFG reachability is conservative around try/finally and empty multi-successor blocks. [Provenance: `PHPVisitorCheck.java`; `doc/CFG.md`]
- Cross-file semantics index PHP files selected by the scanner only; dependencies outside the indexed project and dynamic names may remain unresolved. [Provenance: `SymbolScanner.java`; `ProjectSymbolData.java`]
- Coverage imports line hits only; the read `type` attribute does not produce branch conditions. PHPUnit test execution imports aggregate per-file measures, not individual test-case records. [Provenance: coverage and test importers]
- External issue imports require plugin API 7.2 or newer; the plugin manifest requires SonarQube 9.9, and the pinned runtime is 26.8.0.126808. [Provenance: `ExternalReportWildcardProvider.java`; `sonar-php-plugin/build.gradle.kts`]
- The Community baseline’s built-in `Sonar way` profile is exactly the keys marked default in the native catalog. The profile loader has a reflective hook for another security-rule provider, but no such production rule catalog is part of this assigned Community analyzer, so no behavior or keys are specified for it. [Provenance: `PHPProfileDefinition.java`; `Sonar_way_profile.json`; `manifest/component-inventory.md`]

## 14. Acceptance scenarios

1. **Default recognition.** Given indexed files ending in each default suffix with arbitrary case, analysis assigns language `php`; a file with an unrelated suffix is not selected. When the configured suffix list contains only blanks, the six defaults remain effective.
2. **Exclusion precedence.** Given a PHP file under `vendor`, the default exclusion rejects it before parsing. Given a nonexcluded generated single-line file whose post-header average exceeds 220 characters, the generated-file filter also rejects it.
3. **PHP 8.4 grammar.** Given valid code using asymmetric set visibility, property hooks, final promoted properties, and direct member access after construction, parsing succeeds and exposes the corresponding typed declaration/expression nodes without a version setting.
4. **Parse isolation.** Given one malformed and one valid PHP file with S2260 active, the malformed file receives the parser issue and analysis error but no normal outputs; the valid file is still analyzed. With fail-fast enabled for a nonrecognition analyzer exception, the scanner instead aborts. A `StackOverflowError` from any rule aborts the sensor whether fail-fast is false or true.
5. **Two-pass semantics.** Given a use in one file and a declaration in another indexed file, the symbol prepass makes the project declaration available to the second-pass semantic model. If the declaration file cannot be parsed in the prepass, the other file still analyzes with that symbol unresolved.
6. **Main versus test.** Given a scanner-classified TEST file containing a recognized PHPUnit test case, only PHPUnit-aware rules run; general main-file rules, measures, and CPD are absent, while syntax/symbol highlighting is present outside SonarLint. For a MAIN file under a test-like path, S2068/S6418 suppression applies only while `sonar.tests` is unset or blank and `sonar.testFileHeuristic.disabled` is false; a nonblank `sonar.tests` value prevents suppression.
7. **Framework adaptation.** Given a Drupal or WordPress-detected file and the default S100 format, underscore-style function names accepted by the framework convention are not reported. An explicit S100 `format` override takes precedence over that adaptation.
8. **Rule parameter.** Given S103 active with `maximumLineLength=80`, a source line longer than 80 characters produces a line issue; a line of exactly 80 does not.
9. **Regex literal boundary.** Given a supported `preg_*` call with a statically recoverable literal pattern that violates an active regex rule, the issue range maps to the offending pattern substring. The same pattern assembled dynamically is outside regex-rule analysis.
10. **php.ini.** Given an indexed `php.ini` omitting a directive required by an active php.ini rule, the rule emits a file-level issue. Given a present unsafe directive, it emits on that directive’s line. A malformed second php.ini does not erase findings from the first.
11. **Coverage partial result.** Given two coverage reports, one valid and one malformed, line hits from the valid report are saved; the malformed report adds a warning only. Out-of-range lines and unresolved paths are skipped without discarding valid entries.
12. **PHPUnit execution.** Given nested `testsuites` whose cases resolve to an indexed PHP TEST file, aggregate live-test, skipped, error, failure, and millisecond-duration measures are saved on that file. A path resolving only to a MAIN file produces no test measures and is warned as unresolved.
13. **PHPStan fallback.** Given a configured PHPStan report with a file message, the importer creates `external_phpstan:phpstan.finding` at the reported line. Missing `files` or an empty-array `files` member yields no issue and no crash.
14. **Psalm mapping.** Given a Psalm issue with a known rule ID and valid text range, the exact `external_psalm` key and range are used. An unknown ID uses `external_psalm:psalm.finding`; a missing message is skipped.
15. **Suppression.** Given an issue whose primary start line lies within a matching quoted `SuppressWarnings` range, it is omitted. A different rule key or a location outside the range remains reportable. A `NOSONAR` comment registers its line with the platform filter.
16. **Unchanged cache.** Given status SAME, an equal content hash, compatible cached symbol/CPD data, and checks that permit scan-without-parsing, the analyzer restores cached data and avoids parsing. Any failed prerequisite falls back to a normal parse.
17. **Custom check lifecycle.** Given a registered custom repository and active custom rule, the check initializes once, analyzes every applicable parsed file with AST/symbol context, emits its declared location/secondaries/cost, and terminates once; its exception follows the same per-file fail-fast policy.
18. **SonarLint mode.** Given the same source in SonarLint, native applicable rules run except S5911, while report sensors, file measures, CPD, syntax highlighting, symbol output, and unchanged-file optimization are absent.
